By integrating with Cloudflare Access, companies can use identity-driven budgets and policies.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;There isn&amp;#39;t a CIO on the planet not worried about AI spend right now. CFOs are increasingly nervous, too.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For fear of falling behind, many companies have pushed their employees to use AI as aggressively as possible. The edict was clear: &amp;quot;Move fast, we&amp;#39;ll figure out the bill later.&amp;quot; And for the most part, it worked: AI has been genuinely transformational for the teams that leaned in.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But the costs are real: weâve heard countless horror stories of huge bills and painful overages on token spend.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Today, we&amp;#39;re announcing spend controls in Cloudflare AI Gateway, and a closed beta for identity-driven budgets and routing using Cloudflare Access and your existing identity provider.&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;As weâve spoken with hundreds of companies about their AI strategy, weâve seen a common story:Â  The company gives every engineer access to frontier models through a shared API key. Usage takes off. At the end of the month, finance pulls the invoice and nobody can explain where the money went. Was it the machine learning team training a new pipeline? Was it an intern running Claude Opus on email triage? Was it a runaway continuous integration job that burned through 50 million tokens in a weekend? Nobody knows, because the API key doesn&amp;#39;t tell you who used it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Without guidelines, staff will generally reach for the biggest model available. And why wouldn&amp;#39;t they? If there&amp;#39;s no budget, no visibility, and no routing logic, the rational move is to use the most powerful model for everything. The problem is that most tasks don&amp;#39;t need a frontier model. A code review summary doesn&amp;#39;t need the same model as a complex architecture refactor. A log parser doesn&amp;#39;t need the same model as a customer-facing content generator. It should be easy to select the &lt;i&gt;right&lt;/i&gt; tool for the job, rather than defaulting to the most powerful and expensive one. And it should be simple to see where the spend is going.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can&amp;#39;t calculate ROI on your AI spend without visibility on what you&amp;#39;re spending, and you can&amp;#39;t protect that ROI without controls. Every other line item in a business has a budget and per-team attribution and AI spend should be no different.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;what-ai-gateway-is\&quot;&gt;What AI Gateway isÂ &lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-ai-gateway-is\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI Gateway&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; sits between your applications and AI providers. Instead of calling OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, or any other provider directly, your requests route through AI Gateway first.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This immediately gives you several useful tools:Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Unified billing to easily switch between different providers and models&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Logging across all providers â every request, token count, and cost in one placeÂ &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Response cachingÂ &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Rate limitingÂ &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/features/guardrails/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Content guardrails&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and the ability to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/features/dlp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;block Personally Identifiable Information (PII) and secrets&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; before they reach the model&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;However, AI Gateway didnât have an easy way to answer who is spending what or how you might set limits on AI spend.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You could see aggregate usage across your account. But you couldn&amp;#39;t see that Jane from engineering burned through \\$2,000 on Claude this month while the entire data science team only used \\$400. You couldn&amp;#39;t set a budget that said &amp;quot;engineering gets \\$5,000/month on frontier models, interns get \\$200/month on Kimi K2.6.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;That changes today.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;spend-limits-budgets-for-ai-usage\&quot;&gt;Spend limits: budgets for AI usage&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#spend-limits-budgets-for-ai-usage\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;AI Gateway now supports &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/features/spend-limits\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;spend limits&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; as a core feature. These are true cost control measures in the form of budgets set in dollars, not tokens, that track cumulative spend across all requests, operating independently of traditional rate limiting.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can scope limits to any combination of dimensions: model, provider, or admin-defined custom attributes like user, team, or application. Windows can be fixed (resets on the first of the month, Monday, or midnight) or rolling, and set to daily, weekly, or monthly.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6sH9TwbSEn2Ct9UFuC0SAq/1a1330737879497b0e2511ac28baaed5/BLOG-3331_image1.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3331 image1\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;616\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;AI Gateway calculates cost per request based on the model&amp;#39;s pricing, and tracks cumulative spend against your limit in real time. You can easily track your model spend on our analytics dashboard and filter by model, provider, or any custom attribute.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You have options for what happens when the budget limit is reached. AI Gateway will block further requests by default. Or you can set up rules through &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/features/dynamic-routing/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Dynamic Routes&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to route requests to a fallback model after youâve hit a spend limit, so that a hard spending cap wonât kill your engineersâ workflow. Weâre working to add the capability for you to also send alerts when a limit is reached.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Spend limits are available in open beta today for all AI Gateway users across all plans. Configure them in your gateway settings in the dashboard or via the API.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;we-use-this-ourselves\&quot;&gt;We use this ourselves&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#we-use-this-ourselves\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;We&amp;#39;re tracking token costs inside Cloudflare already. Every Cloudflare employee uses AI tools daily, routing millions of requests and billions of tokens per month through AI Gateway. We faced the same question every company faces at this scale: who&amp;#39;s using what, and how do we budget for it?&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We solved this by enabling AI Gateway to add identity to every request. When an employee authenticates via Cloudflare Access, we extract their identity from the JSON Web Token (JWT) and attach it as metadata on the AI Gateway request. This makes per-user token consumption, team-level usage breakdowns, and cost attribution across the organization all visible in one place.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;identity-driven-budgets-and-policies-closed-beta\&quot;&gt;Identity-driven budgets and policies (closed beta)&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#identity-driven-budgets-and-policies-closed-beta\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;In addition to spend limits, today weâre also announcing identity-driven budgets and policies as a closed beta.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Spend limits in AI Gateway let you set budgets by model, provider, or custom attributes. But your application has to pass that metadata, and AI Gateway trusts whatever it receives. For verified, automatic attribution, you need identity.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;When combined with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Access&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, AI Gateway can see who is making each request â not just which account, but which employee, which identity provider (IdP) group, which service, etc.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Here&amp;#39;s what that looks like in practice.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4A3zzjoUQAzSgmy7gIl6gV/8a09d9fef2dadd5ad7972ea53f45c980/BLOG-3331_image2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3331 image2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;914\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4FJqRBDasIHBqAbfvXtXqT/88bfab78247222efee23a5db7b1917dd/BLOG-3331_image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3331 image4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;895\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can set per-user budgets, say \\$500/month for individual contributors and \\$2,000 for senior engineers. When a user hits their limit, requests can be downgraded to a cheaper model or blocked.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can set per-team model policies. For instance, your ML team gets Claude Opus and GPT-4o. The brand design team can access generative image and video models. Interns use open-source models on Workers AI. These policies map directly to your existing IdP groups, the same identity provider groups you already manage.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/serverless/glossary/what-is-ci-cd/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;CI/CD&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; pipelines and autonomous agents, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/service-credentials/service-tokens/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Access service tokens&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; allow you to give each agent a named identity. You can see that your code review bot used 5 million tokens this week while your documentation generator used 500,000. If one agent is running out of control, apply a budget policy without affecting any others.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Every AI Gateway log entry will include the authenticated identity: email, IdP group, service token name. Export these to your analytics platform, and you&amp;#39;ve got a cost-by-user-by-team breakdown without building anything custom.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Under the hood, you create a Cloudflare Access application for your AI Gateway endpoint and configure policies based on your IdP groups. When a developer or agent makes a request, they authenticate via OAuth, using the typical CLI device-code flow. AI Gateway validates the token and extracts the identity. You don&amp;#39;t need to write a custom Worker, parse JWTs yourself, or rely on honor-system metadata headers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We recently wrote about &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/internal-ai-engineering-stack/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;how we built our internal AI engineering stack&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This is what we are making available today â so you can use it, too, and you don&amp;#39;t have to build it yourself.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you would like access to the closed beta, &lt;a href=\&quot;http://www.cloudflare.com/lp/ai-gateway-identity-aware-endpoints-beta/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;sign up here&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;whats-next-from-cost-control-to-cost-optimization\&quot;&gt;What&amp;#39;s next: from cost control to cost optimization&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#whats-next-from-cost-control-to-cost-optimization\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Setting a budget is necessary. But once youâve got a budget, how do you make the most of it?Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The reality is that not every request needs a frontier model: a summarization task can run on a smaller, cheaper model without meaningful quality loss, while a large-scale code refactor might require the bleeding edge. But without controls, people will almost always opt for the most advanced model.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;A solution for that is coming next: We&amp;#39;re building intelligent, task-based routing in AI Gateway. For each request, we can analyze and automatically route it to the model that will give you the best result at the lowest cost. This is in active development, so follow our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;developer docs&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/changelog/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;changelog&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;get-started\&quot;&gt;Get started&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#get-started\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Itâs free to get started with AI Gateway. Vite stays open source, vendor-agnostic, and built for everyone.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;VoidZero, the company behind &lt;a href=\&quot;https://vite.dev/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Vite&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://vitest.dev/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Vitest&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://rolldown.rs/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Rolldown&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://oxc.rs/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Oxc&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://voidzero.dev/posts/announcing-vite-plus-alpha/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Vite+&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, is joining Cloudflare. As part of this change, all team members of VoidZero are joining Cloudflare, too.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Before saying anything else, we want to make the most important thing clear: Vite, Vitest, Rolldown, Oxc, and Vite+ will stay open source, vendor-agnostic, and community-driven. Nothing about that changes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare&amp;#39;s mission is to help build a better Internet. And a better Internet is an open Internet. Developers need choice, frameworks need a neutral foundation, and applications need to be portable. It is not reasonable to expect the entire web ecosystem to build around a single vendor. The most important tools and frameworks are portable by design.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite is one of the few foundational tools that the whole JavaScript ecosystem agrees on. It earned that position by being fast, excellent, portable, and vendor-neutral. One of the best ways Cloudflare can help build a better Internet is by investing in that foundational open source toolchain. A toolchain that makes the Internet better for everyone, not just people who use Cloudflare or choose to host with us.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Over the last few years we&amp;#39;ve invested heavily in making Cloudflare the best place to build and run websites, applications, and agents on our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;developer platform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. But ultimately that choice will always be yours. Run your Vite application anywhere you want.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;what-this-means-for-vite\&quot;&gt;What this means for Vite&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-this-means-for-vite\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today&amp;#39;s news gives Vite more resources to keep growing, while the things that make Vite what it is remain the same:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite remains MIT-licensed and open source.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite remains vendor-agnostic. Applications built with Vite run anywhere and will continue to do so.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite&amp;#39;s roadmap continues to be driven by the broader Vite team and community, and continues to be developed in the open.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Evan and the rest of the VoidZero team continue to lead Vite, Vitest, Rolldown, Oxc, and Vite+.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare is committing engineering and resources to those projects, not redirecting them.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;We made the same kind of commitment when &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/astro-joins-cloudflare/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Astro joined Cloudflare&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; earlier this year. Astro is still open source, and still deploys anywhere. The team is still shipping the roadmap they were already shipping.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This commitment matters even more with Vite, because Vite is not one framework. Vite is the foundation underlying so many: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://vuejs.org/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Vue&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/introduction\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;SvelteKit&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://nuxt.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Nuxt&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://astro.build/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Astro&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.solidjs.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Solid&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://qwik.dev/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Qwik&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://angular.dev/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Angular&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://reactrouter.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;React Router&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://tanstack.com/start\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;TanStack Start&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Even &lt;a href=\&quot;https://nextjs.org/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Next.js&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; now has a Vite-based implementation in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/vinext/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;vinext&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Vite has become a shared foundation for the JavaScript ecosystem.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our number one goal is to maintain the trust that has earned Vite so much adoption. Not with our words here, but by proving it every day in how we support and develop these projects.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also want to put our money where our mouth is when it comes to our support for open source and shared ecosystem foundations. As part of this announcement, Cloudflare is committing $1 million to a Vite ecosystem fund to support maintainers and contributors, administered by the Vite core team. Vite is bigger than VoidZero or Cloudflare, and the people who have helped build it should be part of what comes next.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;vite-as-the-foundation\&quot;&gt;Vite as the foundation&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#vite-as-the-foundation\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Vite and Cloudflare teams have been collaborating well before this announcement, starting in 2024 with the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://vite.dev/guide/api-environment\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Vite Environment API&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. The Environment API lets Vite run server code in something other than Node.js during development. We worked closely with the Vite team on its design, and then built the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-the-cloudflare-vite-plugin/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Vite plugin&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; on top of it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;When you run &lt;code&gt;vite dev&lt;/code&gt; with the Cloudflare plugin, your server code runs inside &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/workerd\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;workerd&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, the same open-source runtime that powers Workers in production. Durable Objects, D1, KV, R2, Workflows, Workers AI, Agents, Service Bindings, Workers RPC â all of it runs locally inside the same runtime model as production.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For a long time, the cost of developing on a non-Node runtime was that local dev felt like a worse version of production. The Environment API removed that cost without forcing anyone to adopt a Cloudflare-specific dev server. Any runtime that wants to plug into Vite can do the same thing. That kind of design â a generic mechanism in Vite with provider-specific implementations â has proven to work well and is one we want to keep building on.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We knew we were on to something when we saw adoption of the Cloudflare Vite plugin take off:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5gfeiRZUa5M8NfzsnMJ4FA/99600ef4053942f890316e9145a5cc72/BLOG-VOID_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-VOID 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1649\&quot; height=\&quot;954\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite&amp;#39;s adoption curve is one of the more remarkable things to watch in the ecosystem right now. As of this writing, Vite is at roughly &lt;b&gt;129M weekly downloads&lt;/b&gt;. The Cloudflare Vite plugin (&lt;code&gt;@cloudflare/vite-plugin&lt;/code&gt;) is at almost &lt;b&gt;14M weekly downloads&lt;/b&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you had told us a year ago that a Cloudflare Vite plugin would reach downloads equivalent to more than 10% of Vite itself, we wouldnât have believed you. What happened? AI happened. More software is being created than ever before, and a lot of it starts with AI-generated code. Those applications need a default stack and a place to run. Agent-coded applications are choosing Vite, and increasingly they are choosing Vite running on Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;ai-is-changing-how-we-write-software\&quot;&gt;AI is changing how we write software&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#ai-is-changing-how-we-write-software\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Developers used to be the only users of dev servers, bundlers, linters, formatters, and CLIs. That is no longer true: agents are using them too, constantly. They scaffold projects, run dev servers, read errors, write tests, lint and format code, deploy previews, and iterate.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;A lot of AI-generated applications already start as Vite apps, because Vite is fast, well understood, and broadly compatible with what agents have seen in their training data. Fast feedback loops have always been important. They become even more critical when writing software with agents:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Fast builds, because they iterate more than humans do.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Fast tests, because they re-run the suite constantly to verify their own work.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Fast linting and formatting, because those tools become guardrails.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Clear, structured errors, because the agent has to read and act on them.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Consistent CLIs, because small inconsistencies cause big detours.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;The entire VoidZero toolchain is built for this kind of loop. Vitest, Rolldown, Oxc, Oxlint, and Oxfmt are each among the fastest tools in their respective categories, and they work well when they are run over and over by an agent. Vite+ brings those pieces together into one toolchain, with one CLI, one configuration model, and fewer moving parts. That makes the development loop easier for people to understand, and easier for agents to drive reliably.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We are dogfooding this ourselves. The Cloudflare dashboard is built on Vite. Oxlint is already &lt;a href=\&quot;https://x.com/rozenmd/status/2037582692355621289?s=46&amp;amp;t=tJveHCYtiY5v-kdgTMClJQ\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;saving days of engineering time&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in Cloudflare codebases. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.flueframework.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Flue&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, the agent harness framework from the Astro team, is also moving onto Vite as its foundation. Flue can run agents on Node.js, Cloudflare Workers, GitHub Actions, GitLab CI/CD, and more, and the Cloudflare target now uses the official Cloudflare Vite plugin and workerd integration. Vite is becoming the default application foundation inside Cloudflare too.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;vite-is-becoming-full-stack\&quot;&gt;Vite is becoming full-stack&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#vite-is-becoming-full-stack\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;A few years ago, the job of a build tool was straightforward: take source files, produce a bundle, hand it off. That is not enough for modern applications, especially in a world where some of those applications are agents themselves.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;A modern application is server-rendered routes, APIs, background jobs, queues, databases, object storage, real-time, auth, plus a growing list of agents and AI capabilities. The &amp;quot;build&amp;quot; is no longer the end of the story. It is the start of a deployment that has to understand all of those pieces.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;That means Vite has to become more than a build tool. It needs to understand more of the application, while staying true to what made Vite work in the first place: speed, simplicity, and portability.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://void.cloud/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Void&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a deployment platform designed for Vite, has been another testbed for these ideas. It helped explore what a modern application framework should own, what deployment should feel like, and how much of the full application lifecycle can be unified around one toolchain. We have learned a lot from that work.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Now the work is putting those lessons in the right place. Some belong in Vite itself as provider-agnostic primitives: first-class abstractions and hooks for backends, APIs, agents, and deployment that any provider can implement. Other lessons belong inside Cloudflare. Cloudflare will provide a first-class implementation of those hooks on Workers and the rest of our Developer Platform.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Even though some Vite maintainers are joining Cloudflare, changes to Vite itself will continue to go through the same open contribution process as any other Vite contribution. Features added to Vite itself should not be Cloudflare-specific. They will work anywhere Vite works.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;moving-cloudflare-toward-vite\&quot;&gt;Moving Cloudflare toward Vite&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#moving-cloudflare-toward-vite\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The same principle shaped how we think about the future of Cloudflare&amp;#39;s own tooling. We are not moving Vite in the direction of Cloudflare. We are doing the opposite: moving Cloudflare&amp;#39;s application tooling onto Vite, so it is built on top of the same workflows developers already know.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We recently shipped a technical preview of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/cf-cli-local-explorer/\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;cf&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a new unified CLI for the whole Cloudflare platform. Vite is going to be the foundation of our CLI experience for applications. The end goal is one consistent CLI for all of Cloudflare, with the same ergonomics whether you are working on Workers, R2, D1, Agents, or anything else.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If we do this right, the Cloudflare CLI should feel like Vite, not like a separate thing bolted on next to Vite.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;cf dev&lt;/code&gt; should be a superset of &lt;code&gt;vite dev&lt;/code&gt;. Same speed, same hot module replacement, same plugin model, plus the Cloudflare runtime and bindings when you want them.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;cf build&lt;/code&gt; should understand Vite projects natively, without an adapter dance.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;cf deploy&lt;/code&gt; should make deploying a Vite app to Cloudflare simple.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you are running Vite today, the path to Cloudflare will feel like swapping in a superset of the commands you already know. Same project shape. Same Vite workflows. The entire Cloudflare developer platform available when you want it.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;what-happens-next\&quot;&gt;What happens next&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-happens-next\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;In the short term, nothing changes for Vite users or the frameworks building on top of Vite:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite, Vitest, Rolldown, Oxc, and Vite+ keep shipping. The VoidZero team keeps contributing and leading them.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Cloudflare Vite plugin keeps improving.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Environment API and the broader story of &amp;quot;run your server code in the right runtime locally&amp;quot; keeps getting better, including for non-Cloudflare runtimes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Longer term:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;We start the work on moving the Cloudflare CLI toward an experience built directly on top of Vite.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite will get new, clean, provider-agnostic primitives for full-stack apps and agents that work for everyone on any platform.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Over time, we intend to open-source the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://void.cloud/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Void platform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, so others can learn from it and build their own platforms on top of Vite and Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;We will do all of this in public and with the community. The same way Vite has always been built.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;welcome-voidzero\&quot;&gt;Welcome VoidZero&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#welcome-voidzero\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Vite, Vitest, Rolldown, Oxc, and Vite+ exist because a deep ecosystem of open source contributors put years of work into them. These projects are already foundational to how the web is built, and we are grateful to everyone who helped get them here. Thank you to everyone who has contributed code, reviews, issues, docs, plugins, integrations, and support along the way.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We are excited to welcome the VoidZero team to Cloudflare, and excited to put more resources behind these projects. Our job now is to help them grow, stay open, and power the JavaScript ecosystem for everyone.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Vite keeps being Vite. Cloudflare gets to help.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you want to try Vite on Cloudflare today, run:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;npm create vite@latest&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;npx
